##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ते । इत् । निण्यं । हृदयस्य । प्रकेतैः । सहस्रऽवल्शं । अभि । सं । चरंति ।  
> यमेन । ततं । परिधिं । वयंतः । अप्सरसः । उप । सेदुः । वसिष्ठाः ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> त इत्त एव वसिष्ठा निण्यं तिरोहितं दुर्ज्ञानं । निण्यं सस्वरित्यंतर्हितनामसु पाठात् । सहस्रवल्यं सहस्रशाखं संसारं हृदयस्य प्रकेतैः प्रज्ञानैरभि सं चरंति । एवं स्वाच्छंद्येनाभिसंचरंतस्ते वसिष्ठा यमेन कारणात्मना सर्वनियंत्रा ततं विस्तृतं परिधिं वस्त्रं । परिधिरित्यनेन जन्मादिप्रवाहो विवक्षितः । तं वयंतोऽप्सरसो जननीत्वेनोप सेदुः । अत्र वसिष्ठा इति बहुवचनं पूजायां । वसिष्ठः पूर्वं प्रजापतेरुत्पन्नं देहमुत्सृज्याप्सरःसु जायेयेति बुद्धिमकरोदिति भावः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Those very Vasiṣṭhas move about, with the insights (प्रकेतैः (*praketaiḥ*)) of the heart, in the hidden (निण्यं (*niṇyaṃ*), concealed, hard to know) thousand-branched saṃsāra; he notes that निण्यं (*niṇyaṃ*) is read among the names meaning "hidden". Thus roaming at will, those Vasiṣṭhas, weaving the garment (परिधिं (*paridhiṃ*)) spread out by Yama, who as the causal self controls all, approached the Apsarases as mothers. By "garment" the stream of birth and the rest is meant. The plural *Vasiṣṭhas* is used in honour. The sense is that Vasiṣṭha, formerly born of Prajāpati, gave up that body and conceived the thought "may I be born among the Apsarases".

### Printed p. 137

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ते इत् वसिष्ठाः (*te it vasiṣṭhāḥ*)—those very Vasiṣṭhas
- निण्यं (*niṇyaṃ*)—the saṃsāra that is secret, beyond the knowledge of others
- सहस्रवल्शं (*sahasravalśaṃ*)—and has thousands of branches
- हृदयस्य (*hṛdayasya*)—of their heart
- प्रकेतैः (*praketaiḥ*)—with insights
- अभि सं चरंति (*abhi saṃ caraṃti*)—enter and move about everywhere
- यमेन (*yamena*)—by Yama, the controller of the world
- ततं (*tataṃ*)—spread out widely
- परिधिं (*paridhiṃ*)—the garment (the stream of birth and the rest)
- वयंतः (*vayaṃtaḥ*)—taking on
- अप्सरसः (*apsarasaḥ*)—in the womb of an Apsaras
- उपसेदुः (*upaseduḥ*)—have been born

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Those very Vasiṣṭhas, of great glory, with the insights of their heart enter and move about everywhere in the saṃsāra, which is secret, beyond the knowledge of others, and has thousands of branches. They entered the stream of birth and the rest, spread out widely by Yama, the controller of the world, and were born in the womb of an Apsaras.

##### English translation
By the wisdom seated in the heart the Vashisthas traverse the hidden thonsand-branched world, and the Apsaras sit down, wearing the vesture spread out by Yama.

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वि॒द्युतो॒ ज्योतिः॒ परि॑ सं॒जिहा॑नं मि॒त्रावरु॑णा॒ यदप॑श्यतां त्वा  
> तत्ते॒ जन्मो॒तैकं॑ वसिष्ठा॒गस्त्यो॒ यत्त्वा॑वि॒श आ॑ज॒भार॑ ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> विद्युतः । ज्योतिः । परि । संजिहानं । मित्रावरुणा । यत् । अपश्यतां । त्वा ।  
> तत् । ते । जन्म । उत । एकं । वसिष्ठ । अगस्त्यः । यत् । त्वा । विशः । आजभार ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> एतास्वृक्षु वसिष्ठस्यैव देहपरिग्रहः प्रतिपाद्यते । एताश्चेंद्रस्य वाक्यमित्येके वर्णयंत्यपरे वसिष्ठपुत्राणामिति । हे वसिष्ठ यद्यदा विद्युतो विद्युत इव स्वीयं ज्योतिर्देहांतरपरिग्रहार्थं परि संजिहानं परित्यजंतं त्वा त्वां ॥ छांदसमात्मनेपदं ॥ यद्वा । जिघृक्षितदेहार्थं स्वीयं ज्योतिः परि संजिहानं । परिजिघृक्षंत-मित्यर्थः ॥ अस्मिन्पक्षे जहातेर्गर्त्यर्थत्वादात्मनेपदं छांदसं न भवति ॥ मित्रावरुणा मित्रावरुणावपश्यतां । आवाभ्यामयं जायेतेति समकल्पयतामित्यर्थः । तत्तदा ते

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In these Ṛks the taking of a body by Vasiṣṭha himself is set forth. Some explain these Ṛks as the words of Indra; others, as of Vasiṣṭha's sons. O Vasiṣṭha, when Mitra and Varuṇa saw you, giving up (परि संजिहानं (*pari saṃjihānaṃ*)) your own light like lightning in order to take another body, the middle voice being Vedic; or alternatively, your own light desiring to take the body, meaning "wishing to take"; on this view the middle voice is not Vedic, since the root *jahāti* has the sense of motion. Mitra and Varuṇa saw you: that is, they resolved "may he be born from us two". Then that was your one birth **NOTE**.

### Printed p. 138

> केवैकं जन्म । उतापि च यद्यदागस्त्यो विशो निवेशनान्मित्रावरुणावावां जनयिष्याव इत्येतस्मात्पूर्वावस्थानात्त्वामाजभार आजहार ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

And also, when Agastya, from the settling (विशः (*viśaḥ*), from entering) in the earlier state, "we two, Mitra and Varuṇa, shall beget you", brought you here (आजभार (*ājabhāra*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वसिष्ठ (*vasiṣṭha*)—O Vasiṣṭha
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- विद्युतः (*vidyutaḥ*)—of your own radiance
- ज्योतिः (*jyotiḥ*)—the light
- परि संजिहानं (*pari saṃjihānaṃ*)—that was given up
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- मित्रावरुणा (*mitrāvaruṇā*)—Mitra and Varuṇa
- अपश्यतां (*apaśyatāṃ*)—saw
- उत (*uta*)—and
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- अगस्त्यः (*agastyaḥ*)—Agastya
- विशः (*viśaḥ*)—from your former dwelling
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- आ जभार (*ā jabhāra*)—brought
- तत् (*tat*)—then
- ते (*te*)—your
- एकं जन्म (*ekaṃ janma*)—one birth came about

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Vasiṣṭha, when, for another birth, you gave up your former radiance and Mitra and Varuṇa saw you, and when Agastya brought you from your former dwelling place, to be born of Mitra and Varuṇa along with himself, then one birth of yours came about.

##### English translation
When Mitra and Varuna beheld you, Vasistha, quitting the lustre of the lightning (for the different form), then one of your births (took place), in as much as Agastya bore you from your (former) abode.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk and in some of the following Ṛks the account of the birth of the sage Vasiṣṭha is told. In this Ṛk there is mention of the question of the births of the sages Vasiṣṭha and Agastya. Some maintain that the words in these Ṛks were spoken by Indra himself, and others that they were spoken by the sons of Vasiṣṭha. The details of the birth of the sage Vasiṣṭha we have explained at length in the Special Topics of the next Ṛk.

#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒तासि॑ मैत्रावरु॒णो व॑सिष्ठो॒र्वश्या॑ ब्रह्म॒न्मन॒सोऽधि॑ जा॒तः  
> द्र॒प्सं स्क॒न्नं ब्रह्म॑णा॒ दैव्ये॑न॒ विश्वे॑ दे॒वाः पुष्क॑रे त्वाददंत ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उत । असि । मैत्रावरुणः । वसिष्ठ । उर्वश्याः । ब्रह्मन् । मनसः । अधि । जातः ।  
> द्रप्सं । स्कन्नं । ब्रह्मणा । दैव्येन । विश्वे । देवाः । पुष्करे । त्वा । अददंत ॥ ११ ॥

### Printed p. 139

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उतापि च हे वसिष्ठ मैत्रावरुणो मित्रावरुणयोः पुत्रोऽसि । हे ब्रह्मन् वसिष्ठ उर्वश्या अप्सरसो मनसो ममायं पुत्रः स्यादितीदृशात्संकल्पाद्द्रप्सं रेतो मित्रावरुणयोरुर्वशीदर्शनात्स्कन्नमासीत् । तस्मादधि जातोऽसि । तथा च वक्ष्यते सत्रे ह जातावित्यृचि । एवं जातं त्वा त्वां दैव्येन देवसंबंधिना ब्रह्मणा वेदराशिनाहंभुवा युक्तं पुष्करे विश्वे देवा आददंत । अधारयंत ॥ तथा चादितेर्मित्रावरुणौ जज्ञाते इति प्रकृत्य पठ्यते । तयोरादित्ययोः सत्रे दृष्ट्वाप्सरसमुर्वशीं । रेतश्चस्कंद तत्कुंभे न्यपतद्वासतीवरे ॥ तेनैव तु मुहूर्तेन वीर्यवंतौ तपस्विनौ । अगस्त्यश्च वसिष्ठश्च तत्रर्षी संबभूवतुः ॥ बहुधा पतितं रेतः कलशे च जले स्थले । स्थले वसिष्ठस्तु मुनिः संभूत ऋषिसत्तमः । कुंभे त्वगस्त्यः संभूतो जले मत्स्यो महाद्युतिः । उदियाय ततोऽगस्त्यः शम्यामात्रो महातपाः ॥ मानेन संमितो यस्मात्तस्मान्मान्य इहोच्यते । यद्वा कुंभादृषिर्जातः कुंभेनापि हि मीयते । कुंभ इत्यभिधानं च परिमाणस्य लक्ष्यते । ततोऽप्सु गृह्यमाणासु वसिष्ठः पुष्करे स्थितः । सर्वतः पुष्करे तं हि विश्वेदेवा आधारयन् ॥ बृ. ५-२८३ रिंद २८९ ॥ इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: "And also, Vasiṣṭha, you are Maitrāvaruṇa, the son of Mitra and Varuṇa. O Brahman Vasiṣṭha, from the thought of Urvaśī the Apsaras, 'May this be my son', and from the sight of Urvaśī, the seed (द्रप्सं (*drapsaṃ*)) of Mitra and Varuṇa was spilled. From that you were born." As will be said in the Ṛk beginning *satre ha jātau*. Thus born, you, endowed with the divine Brahman (the mass of the Veda, self-existent), were held (आददंत (*ādadaṃta*), "supported") by all the gods in the lake (पुष्करे (*puṣkare*)).

He then quotes, with a reference, the verses that state this about Aditi's sons Mitra and Varuṇa. At their sacrificial session they saw the Apsaras Urvaśī, and the seed fell into the jar called Vasatīvara. In that same moment the two powerful ascetic seers, Agastya and Vasiṣṭha, came into being there. The seed fell in many ways: in the jar, in the water and on the ground. On the ground the sage Vasiṣṭha, best of seers, arose; in the jar Agastya arose; in the water a great shining fish. Then Agastya, of great austerity, rose up, measuring a śamyā. Because he was measured by a measure (मानेन (*mānena*)) he is called Mānya here; or else the seer was born of the jar (कुंभ (*kuṃbha*)), and by a jar one measures, for "kumbha" is a name for a measure. Then, as the waters were being taken up, Vasiṣṭha stood on the lotus (पुष्करे (*puṣkare*)), and the Viśvedevas held him there on every side. The reference is given as बृ. ५-२८३ रिंद २८९ (*bṛ. 5-283 riṃda 289*) (Bṛ. 5-283 to 289).**NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उत (*uta*)—and
- वसिष्ठ (*vasiṣṭha*)—O Vasiṣṭha
- मैत्रावरुणः (*maitrāvaruṇaḥ*)—you, as son of Mitra and Varuṇa
- असि (*asi*)—are
- ब्रह्मन् (*brahman*)—O Vasiṣṭha, possessed of knowledge of Brahman
- उर्वश्याः (*urvaśyāḥ*)—of Urvaśī, the Apsaras woman
- मनसः (*manasaḥ*)—from the will (thought)
- द्रप्सं (*drapsaṃ*)—the seed of Mitra and Varuṇa
- स्कन्नं (*skannaṃ*)—was spilled
- अधि जातः (*adhi jātaḥ*)—you were born from that
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you, thus born
- दैव्येन (*daivyena*)—divine
- ब्रह्मणा (*brahmaṇā*)—seeing you endowed with the mass of the Veda
- विश्वे देवाः (*viśve devāḥ*)—all the gods
- पुष्करे (*puṣkare*)—on the lotus leaf
- अददंत (*adadaṃta*)—established (you)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Vasiṣṭha, you are the son of Mitra and Varuṇa. O Vasiṣṭha, possessed of knowledge of Brahman, through the will of Urvaśī, the Apsaras woman, the seed of Mitra and Varuṇa was spilled. You were born from it. Seeing you, thus born, endowed with the divine mass of the Veda, all the gods established you on the lotus leaf.

##### English translation
Verily, Vasishtha, you are the son of Mitra and Varuna, born, Brahman, of the will of Urvashi, after the seminal effusion : all the gods have sustained you, (endowed) with celestial and Vaidic vigour in the lake.

### Printed p. 140

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The birth of Mitra and Varuṇa from Aditi, the mother of the gods, is stated in many places; we have explained this in the pages of the Ṛ. Saṃ. volume. Here we write down in order what Yāska says in the Nirukta, and what Śaunaka says in the Bṛhaddevatā, on how Vasiṣṭha and the seer Agastya were born of Mitra and Varuṇa. Because the story of these seers' birth is connected with the Apsaras woman Urvaśī, and because the word Urvaśī is read in this Ṛk, Yāska's explanation of the derivation of the word Urvaśī is first cited.—

उर्वश्यप्सरा उर्वभ्यश्नुते उरुभ्यामश्नुत उरुर्वा वशोऽस्याः । अप्सरा अप्सारिण्यपि वाप्स इति रूपनामाप्सातेरप्सानीयं भवत्यादर्शनीयं व्यापनीयं वा स्पष्टं दर्शनायेति शाकपूणिः । यदप्स इत्यभक्षस्य अप्नो नामेति व्यापिनः । तद्वा भवति रूपवती तेदनयोत्तमिति वा तेदस्यै दत्तमिति वा । तस्या दर्शनान्मित्रावरुणयो रेतश्चस्कंद तदभिवादिन्येषर्ग्भवति ॥ (*urvaśyapsarā urvabhyaśnute urubhyāmaśnuta ururvā vaśo 'syāḥ | apsarā apsāriṇyapi vāpsa iti rūpanāmāpsāterapsānīyaṃ bhavatyādarśanīyaṃ vyāpanīyaṃ vā spaṣṭaṃ darśanāyeti śākapūṇiḥ | yadapsa ityabhakṣasya apno nāmeti vyāpinaḥ | tadvā bhavati rūpavatī tedanayottamiti vā tedasyai dattamiti vā | tasyā darśanānmitrāvaruṇayo retaścaskaṃda tadabhivādinyeṣargbhavati ‖*) (Ni. 5-13)

Urvaśī is an Apsaras woman. The reason this woman has the name Urvaśī is as follows.

(1) उर्वभ्यश्नुते । उरु महत् यशोऽभिव्याप्नोतीति । (*urvabhyaśnute | uru mahat yaśo 'bhivyāpnotīti |*). उरु (*uru*) means abundant fame or renown; अभ्यश्नुते (*abhyaśnute*) means she attains it. This woman is very famous for her great fame (here fame means beauty of form and the like); this is the reason she has the name Urvaśī.

(2) ऊरूभ्यां मैथुने धर्मे पुरुषं अश्नुते व्याप्नोतीति उर्वाशिनी सती उर्वशीत्युच्यते (*ūrūbhyāṃ maithune dharme puruṣaṃ aśnute vyāpnotīti urvāśinī satī urvaśītyucyate*). ऊरु (*ūru*) means thighs. With her attractive thighs this woman pervades the man in the act of union with embraces and the like and gives him delight. Therefore she has the name Urvāśinī or Urvaśī.

(3) Or उरुः महान् अस्याः वशः कामः सेयमुरुवशिनी सती उर्वशीत्युच्यते (*uruḥ mahān asyāḥ vaśaḥ kāmaḥ seyamuruvaśinī satī urvaśītyucyate*). उरुः (*uruḥ*) means much, great; वशः (*vaśaḥ*) means desire. Since this woman has exceedingly great desire, she has the name Uruvaśinī or Urvaśī. Thus three explanations of the word Urvaśī are given.

Now he gives the explanation of the word अप्सराः (*apsarāḥ*) in order to set out its meaning. Although this word is not read in this Ṛk, it had to be mentioned by way of the context when explaining the word Urvaśī. Therefore the explanation of this word is stated here.

(1) *apsarāḥ | sā hi apsāriṇī bhavati | apaḥ patati nityameva sarati | tatprabhava

### Printed p. 141

tvāt tadeva tasyāḥ priyamudakaṃ tasmādapsarāḥ*. अप्सराः (*apsarāḥ*) means अप्सारिणी (*apsāriṇī*). अपः (*apaḥ*) means water: one who moves over water. Therefore water is exceedingly dear to this woman, and so the name Apsarāḥ has come.

(2) Or अद्भ्यः सृतेत्यप्सराः (*adbhyaḥ sṛtetyapsarāḥ*). अपि चैवमन्यथा स्यात् (*api caivamanyathā syāt*). अप्स इति एतत् रूपनाम (*apsa iti etat rūpanāma*). आप्सातेः प्रतिषेधपूर्वस्य प्सातेः भक्षणार्थस्य (*āpsāteḥ pratiṣedhapūrvasya psāteḥ bhakṣaṇārthasya*). तद्धि अप्सानीयं आदर्शनीयं भवति (*taddhi apsānīyaṃ ādarśanīyaṃ bhavati*). न हि तद्भक्ष्यते (*na hi tadbhakṣyate*). किं तर्हि (*kiṃ tarhi*). आदर्शनीयं तदाभिमुख्येन स्थित्वा द्रष्टव्यमेव भवति चक्षुषा न पुनर्मुखेन भक्षयितव्यमित्यर्थः (*ādarśanīyaṃ tadābhimukhyena sthitvā draṣṭavyameva bhavati cakṣuṣā na punarmukhena bhakṣayitavyamityarthaḥ*). अप्सः (*apsaḥ*) is a name for form. The root प्सा (*psā*) means "eat". अ + प्सा (*a + psā*) means "not fit to be eaten". So the form in this woman is not fit to be eaten, but is fit to be gazed at well with the eyes; that is, the form of this woman is very attractive.

(3) व्यापनीयं वा (*vyāpanīyaṃ vā*). व्यापनमर्हति (*vyāpanamarhati*). आप्लृ व्याप्तौ तस्य वा अप्सः (*āplṛ vyāptau tasya vā apsaḥ*); तद्धि नायनेन रश्मिना आत्मनः प्रत्ययाधानार्थं व्यापयितव्यं भवति (*taddhi nāyanena raśminā ātmanaḥ pratyayādhānārthaṃ vyāpayitavyaṃ bhavati*). स्पष्टं दर्शनायेति शाकपूणिः (*spaṣṭaṃ darśanāyeti śākapūṇiḥ*). From the root आप्लृ व्याप्तौ (*āplṛ vyāptau*) the word अप्सः (*apsaḥ*) is formed, giving the meaning "pervading"; that is, the form pervades and is directly visible, so that all can see it clearly.

All these differences of meaning are according to the view of the teacher Śākapūṇi.

Now he gives another kind of meaning for the word Apsarā. It was said before that अप्सः (*apsaḥ*) means form. Now, as from the root रा दाने (*rā dāne*), the word रा (*rā*) has the sense "giving, given". So Apsarā means that the form this woman has is accepted by her; or, since this form (beauty) was given to her by Brahmā, the name Apsarā has come; this too is the meaning.

At the sight of this Apsaras woman Urvaśī, desire arose in Mitra and Varuṇa and their seed (semen) was spilled. This Ṛk says that from that seed the seers Vasiṣṭha and Agastya were born.

Having thus stated the explanations of the words Urvaśī and Apsarāḥ, he now gives the explanation of the Ṛk at hand.—

अस्यसि मैत्रावरुणो वसिष्ठोर्वश्या ब्रह्मन्मनसोऽधिजातो द्रप्सं स्कन्नं ब्रह्मणा दैव्येन ॥ द्रप्सः संभृतः स्पानीयो भवति सर्वे देवाः पुष्करे त्वाधारयंत । पुष्करमंतरिक्षं पोषति भूतान्यत्र । पुष्करं पूजाकरं पूजयितव्यं वा । इदमपीतरत् पुष्करमेतस्मादेव पुष्करं वपुष्करं वा । पुष्पं पुष्पतेः ॥ (*asyasi maitrāvaruṇo vasiṣṭhorvaśyā brahmanmanaso 'dhijāto drapsaṃ skannaṃ brahmaṇā daivyena ‖ drapsaḥ saṃbhṛtaḥ spānīyo bhavati sarve devāḥ puṣkare tvādhārayaṃta | puṣkaramaṃtarikṣaṃ poṣati bhūtānyatra | puṣkaraṃ pūjākaraṃ pūjayitavyaṃ vā | idamapītarat puṣkarametasmādeva puṣkaraṃ vapuṣkaraṃ vā | puṣpaṃ puṣpateḥ ‖*) (Ni. 5-14)

### Printed p. 142

O Vasiṣṭha, you are certainly the son of Mitra and Varuṇa. O knower of Brahman, you were born from the mind of Urvaśī. On the sacrificial ground, from the sight of Urvaśī, desire arose in Mitra and Varuṇa and their seed was spilled. At that time all the gods, so that the spilled seed of Mitra and Varuṇa should not fall on the earth, praising with mantras made up of Ṛk, Yajus and Sāman, caused that seed to fall on the water in a jar or in the atmosphere. From it the birth of Vasiṣṭha and Agastya took place.

द्रप्सः (*drapsaḥ*) means seed. द्र (*dra*) means flowing; प्सा (*psā*) means eating or bearing. This seed, or semen, flows from the man's organ and is eaten by the woman's womb, that is, absorbed or held. Therefore the seed has got the name द्रप्सः (*drapsaḥ*).

The word पुष्कर (*puṣkara*) has many meanings. Puṣkara means the atmosphere: तद्धि पोषति भूतानि पुष्णात्येवकाशदानेनोपकुर्वन् (*taddhi poṣati bhūtāni puṣṇātyevakāśadānenopakurvan*). Because it gives room to beings and nourishes them, the atmosphere is called puṣkara. Puṣkara also means water. For,—

तेन हि पूजा क्रियते देवादिभ्यः तत्पूजाकारकत्वात् पुष्करं (*tena hi pūjā kriyate devādibhyaḥ tatpūjākārakatvāt puṣkaraṃ*). Or पूजयितव्यं पूजनार्हमात्मन एवेति पुष्करं (*pūjayitavyaṃ pūjanārhamātmana eveti puṣkaraṃ*). With this water people perform worship and the like to the gods; that is, water is used for the rite of worship. Or this water itself is worthy of worship; that is, people worship it. Therefore water is called puṣkara.

Besides this, puṣkara has another meaning, the lotus (flower). This lotus flower too is used for worship and the like; and puṣkara is also वपुष्करं (*vapuṣkaraṃ*), since it is used to adorn the body with this flower. For these reasons the lotus flower is called puṣkara.

By way of the context he also gives the derivation of the word पुष्प (*puṣpa*): पुष्पं पुष्पतेः (*puṣpaṃ puṣpateḥ*). पुष्पतिः विकसनार्थः (*puṣpatiḥ vikasanārthaḥ*). Puṣpa means flower. It blossoms; that is, what was a bud opens well and gives out fragrance. Therefore the flower is called puṣpa.

Having explained all that is said in the Nirukta about this Ṛk, we now explain the manner of Vasiṣṭha's origin in the Ṛgveda's sūkta at hand (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-33-10 to 14), and afterwards the manner of Vasiṣṭha's origin in the work Bṛhaddevatā, spoken by the great sage Śaunaka.—

**The manner of Vasiṣṭha's origin as stated in the Ṛgveda**
In many places in the Ṛk Saṃhitā Vasiṣṭha is praised as born of a divine portion and possessed of personal greatness. The manner of his origin is told as follows.

विद्युतो ज्योतिः परि संजिहानं मित्रावरुणा यदपश्यतां त्वा । (*vidyuto jyotiḥ pari saṃjihānaṃ mitrāvaruṇā yadapaśyatāṃ tvā |*)
तत्ते जन्मोतैकं वसिष्ठागस्त्यो यत्त्वाविश आजभार ॥ (*tatte janmotaikaṃ vasiṣṭhāgastyo yattvāviśa ājabhāra ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-33-10)

### Printed p. 143

O Vasiṣṭha, when you spread your own radiance and displayed your power, Mitra and Varuṇa saw you. Immediately your birth took place on the earth. This is only one of your many manifestations.

उतासि मैत्रावरुणो वसिष्ठोर्वश्या ब्रह्मन् मनसोऽधि जातः । (*utāsi maitrāvaruṇo vasiṣṭhorvaśyā brahman manaso 'dhi jātaḥ |*)
द्रप्सं स्कन्नं ब्रह्मणा दैव्येन विश्वे देवाः पुष्करे त्वाददंते ॥ (*drapsaṃ skannaṃ brahmaṇā daivyena viśve devāḥ puṣkare tvādadaṃte ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-33-11)

O Vasiṣṭha, as soon as Urvaśī was seen, you were born, according to her will, through the power of the seed of Mitra and Varuṇa that had been spilled. Immediately all the gods established you, born of a divine portion and endowed with the mass of the Veda, in the lotus.

स प्रकेत उभयस्य प्रविद्वान्सहस्रदान उत वा सदानः । (*sa praketa ubhayasya pravidvānsahasradāna uta vā sadānaḥ |*)
यमेन ततं परिधिं वयिष्यन्नप्सरसः परि जज्ञे वसिष्ठः ॥ (*yamena tataṃ paridhiṃ vayiṣyannapsarasaḥ pari jajñe vasiṣṭhaḥ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-33-12)

This Vasiṣṭha, fully knowing the principles of both heaven and earth, is a giver of gifts of many kinds, or is himself of the nature of a gift. Covered with the cloth of saṃsāra, spread out widely by Yama, the controller of the whole world, he was born on the earth, in the womb of Urvaśī.

सत्रे ह जाताविषिता नमोभिः कुंभे रेतः सिषिचतुः समानं । (*satre ha jātāviṣitā namobhiḥ kuṃbhe retaḥ siṣicatuḥ samānaṃ |*)
ततो ह मान उदियाय मध्यात्ततो जातमृषिमाहुर्वसिष्ठं ॥ (*tato ha māna udiyāya madhyāttato jātamṛṣimāhurvasiṣṭhaṃ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-33-13)

When Mitra and Varuṇa, consecrated for the sacrifice, poured their seed together into the jar that had been set up with praises, from that jar called Vasatīvarī, first Agastya arose in the middle, and afterwards Vasiṣṭha was born from the same jar.

उक्थभृतं सामभृतं बिभर्ति ग्रावाणं बिभ्रत्प्र वदात्यग्रे । (*ukthabhṛtaṃ sāmabhṛtaṃ bibharti grāvāṇaṃ bibhratpra vadātyagre |*)
उपैनमाध्वं सुमनस्यमाना आ वो गच्छाति प्रतृदो वसिष्ठः ॥ (*upainamādhvaṃ sumanasyamānā ā vo gacchāti pratṛdo vasiṣṭhaḥ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-33-14)

O sacrificers called Pratṛd, the venerable Vasiṣṭha comes to you. Honour such a Vasiṣṭha. In your sacrifice he occupies the place of the Brahman priest and is the guide of the reciters of the śastra mantras, of the Udgātṛs, and of those engaged in the pressing rite (abhiṣava). Therefore honour and respect him: such words of praise are there. The author of the Bṛhaddevatā explains this same matter as follows.

**The manner of Vasiṣṭha's origin as stated in the Bṛhaddevatā**

प्राजापत्यो मरीचिर्हि मारीचः कश्यपो मुनिः । (*prājāpatyo marīcirhi mārīcaḥ kaśyapo muniḥ |*)
तस्य देव्योऽभवज्जाया दाक्षायण्यस्त्रयोदश ॥ (*tasya devyo 'bhavajjāyā dākṣāyaṇyastrayodaśa ‖*)
अदितिर्दितिर्दनुः काला दनायुः सिंहिका मुनिः । (*aditirditirdanuḥ kālā danāyuḥ siṃhikā muniḥ |*)
(Bṛ. De. 5. 143, 144)

### Printed p. 144

Prajāpati had a son named Marīci. Marīci's son was the great sage Kaśyapa. This Kaśyapa married thirteen divine maidens, daughters of Dakṣa. Those maidens bore names such as Aditi, Diti, Danu, Kālā, Danāyu, Siṃhikā.

तत्रैका त्वदितिर्देवी द्वादशाजनयत्सुतान् । (*tatraikā tvaditirdevī dvādaśājanayatsutān |*)
भगश्चैवार्यमांशश्च मित्रो वरुण एव च । (*bhagaścaivāryamāṃśaśca mitro varuṇa eva ca |*)
धाता चैव विधाता च विवस्वांश्च महाद्युतिः ॥ (*dhātā caiva vidhātā ca vivasvāṃśca mahādyutiḥ ‖*)
(Bṛ. De. 5. 146,147)

Among these daughters, Aditi, of divine nature, bore twelve sons: Bhaga, Aryaman, Aṃśa, Mitra, Varuṇa, Dhātṛ, Vidhātṛ, the greatly radiant Vivasvān, and

त्वष्टा पूषा तथैवेंद्रो द्वादशो विष्णुरुच्यते । (*tvaṣṭā pūṣā tathaiveṃdro dvādaśo viṣṇurucyate |*)
द्वंद्वं तस्यास्तु तज्जज्ञे मित्रश्च वरुणश्च ह ॥ (*dvaṃdvaṃ tasyāstu tajjajñe mitraśca varuṇaśca ha ‖*)
(Bṛ. De. 5-148)

Tvaṣṭṛ, Pūṣan, Indra and Viṣṇu: these are the twelve children. Viṣṇu is the twelfth. Thus from this Aditi were born the pair (twins) Mitra and Varuṇa.

तयोरादित्ययोः सत्रे दृष्ट्वाप्सरसमुर्वशीं । (*tayorādityayoḥ satre dṛṣṭvāpsarasamurvaśīṃ |*)
रेतश्चस्कंद तत्कुंभे न्यपतद्वासतीवरे ॥ (*retaścaskaṃda tatkuṃbhe nyapatadvāsatīvare ‖*)
(Bṛ. De. 5-149)

Among these twelve Ādityas, Mitra and Varuṇa both saw the divine woman Urvaśī at the time of a sacrifice of the nature of a sattra. As soon as they saw her their seed was spilled. Then it fell together into the jar called Vasatīvara.

तेनैव तु मुहूर्तेन वीर्यवंतौ तपस्विनौ । (*tenaiva tu muhūrtena vīryavaṃtau tapasvinau |*)
अगस्त्यश्च वसिष्ठश्च तत्रर्षी संबभूवतुः ॥ (*agastyaśca vasiṣṭhaśca tatrarṣī saṃbabhūvatuḥ ‖*)
(Bṛ. De. 5-150)

As soon as that seed fell in that jar, the powerful ascetic seers Agastya and Vasiṣṭha were born from it. The manner of their birth is this:

बहुधा पतिते शुक्रे कलशेऽथ जले स्थले । (*bahudhā patite śukre kalaśe 'tha jale sthale |*)
स्थले वसिष्ठस्तु मुनिः संभूत ऋषिसत्तमः ॥ (*sthale vasiṣṭhastu muniḥ saṃbhūta ṛṣisattamaḥ ‖*)
(Bṛ. De. 5-151)

That seed spilled by Mitra and Varuṇa was divided in many ways. It fell in the jar, in the water and on the earth. From the part that fell on the earth, the great sage Vasiṣṭha, the best of all seers, was born on the earth. (Agastya was born in the jar, and a fish in the water.)

### Printed p. 145
#### Ṛk 12

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स प्र॑के॒त उ॒भय॑स्य प्रवि॒द्वान्स॒हस्र॑दान उ॒त वा॒ सदा॑नः ।  
> य॒मेन॑ त॒तं प॑रि॒धिं व॑यि॒ष्यन्न॑प्स॒रसः॒ परि॑ जज्ञे॒ वसि॑ष्ठः ॥ १२ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । प्रकेतः । उभयस्य । प्रविद्वान् । सहस्रदानः । उत । वा । सदानः ।  
> यमेन । ततं । परिधिं । वयिष्यन् । अप्सरसः । परि । जज्ञे । वसिष्ठः ॥ १२ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> स वसिष्ठः प्रकेतः प्रकृष्टज्ञान उभयस्योभयं दिवं च पृथिवीं च प्रविद्वान् प्रकर्षेण जानन् सहस्रदानोऽभवत् । किमनेन सहस्रदान इति विशेषणेन उत वासि ना सदानः सर्वदानसहित एवाभवत् । किंच वसिष्ठो यमेन कारणात्मना सर्वनियंत्रा ततं विस्तृतं परिधिं वस्त्रं । परिधिरित्यनेन संसारप्रवाहो विवक्षितः । तं वयिष्यन्नप्सरस उर्वश्याः । परीति पंचम्यर्थानुवादः । जज्ञे । जातः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

That Vasiṣṭha was प्रकेतः (*praketaḥ*), one of excellent knowledge. He thoroughly knew उभयस्य (*ubhayasya*), both heaven and earth, and became a giver of a thousand gifts. Sāyaṇa then asks what the qualifier "giver of a thousand" is for, and answers that he was सदानः (*sadānaḥ*), endowed with every gift. **NOTE**. Moreover Vasiṣṭha, being about to weave or put on the spread-out परिधिं (*paridhiṃ*), the garment that Yama, the controller of all, spread as the cause, was born from the Apsaras Urvaśī. By "paridhi" the flow of saṃsāra is meant. परि (*pari*) is a restatement of the sense of the ablative.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सः (*saḥ*)—that same Vasiṣṭha
- प्रकेतः (*praketaḥ*)—one of excellent knowledge
- उभयस्य (*ubhayasya*)—of both heaven and earth, their reality
- प्रविद्वान् (*pravidvān*)—one who knew fully
- सहस्रदानः (*sahasradānaḥ*)—became a giver of things numbering a thousand
- उत वा (*uta vā*)—or
- सदानः (*sadānaḥ*)—became donation itself
- यमेन (*yamena*)—by Yama, the controller of the world
- ततं (*tataṃ*)—spread out widely
- परिधिं (*paridhiṃ*)—the garment in the form of the flow of saṃsāra
- वयिष्यन् (*vayiṣyan*)—wearing
- अप्सरसः (*apsarasaḥ*)—from the Apsaras woman Urvaśī
- परि जज्ञे (*pari jajñe*)—was born

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

That same Vasiṣṭha, of great glory, became one of excellent knowledge, fully knowing the reality of both heaven and earth, and a giver of a thousand things; or else he is donation itself. Wearing the garment, in the form of the flow of saṃsāra, spread out widely by Yama, the controller of the world, he was born from the Apsaras woman Urvaśī.

##### English translation
He, the sage, cognizant of both worlds, was the donor of thousands : he was verily donation : wearing the vesture spread by Yama, Vasishtha was born of the Apsaras.

### Printed p. 146
#### Ṛk 13

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स॒त्रे ह॑ जा॒तावि॑षि॒ता नमो॑भिः कुं॒भे रेतः॑ सिषिचतुः समा॒नं ।  
> ततो॑ ह॒ मान॒ उदि॑याय॒ मध्या॒त्ततो॑ जा॒तमृषि॑माहु॒र्वसि॑ष्ठं ॥ १३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सत्रे । ह । जातौ । इषिता । नमःऽभिः । कुंभे । रेतः । सिसिचतुः । समानं ।  
> ततः । ह । मानः । उत् । इयाय । मध्यात् । ततः । जातं । ऋषिं । आहुः । वसिष्ठं ॥ १३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सत्रे बहुकर्तृके यागे । हेति पूरणः । जातौ दीक्षितौ मित्रावरुणाविषिताध्येषितौ स्वयमन्यैर्जनैर्नमोभिः स्तुतिभिः कुंभे वासतीवरे कलशे समानमेकदैव रेतः सिषिचतुः । आसिंचतां । ततो वासतीवरात्कुंभान्मध्यादगस्त्यो मानः शमीप्रमाण उदियाय । प्रादुर्बभूव । तत एव कुंभाद्वसिष्ठमप्यृषिं जातमाहुः

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

सत्रे (*satre*) means in a sacrifice with many performers; ह (*ha*) is a filler. जातौ (*jātau*) means the two, Mitra and Varuṇa, consecrated. They were इषिता (*iṣitā*), urged on by the other people with नमोभिः (*namobhiḥ*), praises. In the कुंभे (*kuṃbhe*), the jar called Vasatīvara, they poured समानं (*samānaṃ*), at one and the same time, their seed. From that Vasatīvara jar, from its middle, Agastya, of the measure of a śamī (stake), arose, that is, came into being. From that very jar they say that the seer Vasiṣṭha too was born.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सत्रे ह (*satre ha*)—in a sacrifice of the satra kind
- जातौ (*jātau*)—Mitra and Varuṇa, who were consecrated
- नमोभिः (*namobhiḥ*)—by praises
- इषिता (*iṣitā*)—being honoured **NOTE**
- कुंभे (*kuṃbhe*)—in the jar named Vasatīvarī
- समानं (*samānaṃ*)—together
- रेतः (*retaḥ*)—their seed
- सिषिचतुः (*siṣicatuḥ*)—poured
- ततः (*tataḥ*)—from that jar
- मध्यात् (*madhyāt*)—from the middle
- मानः (*mānaḥ*)—Agastya, of the measure of a śamī
- उदियाय (*udiyāya*)—was born
- ततः (*tataḥ*)—from that very one
- जातं (*jātaṃ*)—born
- ऋषिं (*ṛṣiṃ*)—the seer
- वसिष्ठं (*vasiṣṭhaṃ*)—as the seer Vasiṣṭha
- आहुः (*āhuḥ*)—they say

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Mitra and Varuṇa, consecrated in a sacrifice of the satra kind and honoured with praises, gathered their seed together in the jar named Vasatīvarī. From the middle of that jar Agastya, of the measure of a śamī, was born. Another seer born from the same is called the seer Vasiṣṭha.

##### English translation
Consecrated for the sacrifice, propitiated by praises, they, Mitra and Varuna, poured a common effusion into the water jar, from the midst of which Mana uprose, and from which also, they say, Vasishtha was born.

### Printed p. 147
#### Ṛk 14

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒क्थ॒भृतं॑ साम॒भृतं॑ बिभर्ति॒ ग्रावा॑णं॒ बिभ्र॒त्प्र व॑दा॒त्यग्रे॑ ।  
> उपै॑नमाध्वं सुमन॒स्यमा॑ना॒ आ वो॑ गच्छाति प्रतृदो॒ वसि॑ष्ठः ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उक्थभृतं । सामभृतं । बिभर्ति । ग्रावाणं । बिभ्रत् । प्र । वदाति । अग्रे ।  
> उप । एनं । आध्वं । सुमनस्यमानाः । आ । वः । गच्छाति । प्रतृदः । वसिष्ठः ॥ १४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे प्रतृदः । प्रतृद इति तृत्सव एवाभिधीयंते नामांतरेण । वो युष्मान् वसिष्ठ आ गच्छाति । आगच्छति । एनं वसिष्ठं सुमनस्यमानाः सुमनसः संत उपाध्वं । उपतिष्ठत । आगतश्चासौ वसिष्ठो यज्ञेऽग्रे पुरोहितो ब्रह्मा सन्नुक्थभृतं शस्त्राणां संभर्तारं बिभर्ति । सामभृतमुद्गातारं बिभर्ति । ग्रावाणमभिषवणं बिभ्रद्भृतमध्वर्युं च बिभर्ति । प्र वदाति । यज्ञे यत्प्रवदितव्यं भ्रेषादिनिमित्ते कर्तव्यमस्ति तदपि वदतीति तृत्सून् प्रतींद्रो ब्रवीति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

हे प्रतृदः (*he pratṛdaḥ*): "प्रतृदः (*pratṛdaḥ*)" is another name by which the Tṛtsus themselves are meant. Vasiṣṭha is coming to you (आ गच्छाति (*ā gacchāti*) = आगच्छति (*āgacchati*)). Be well disposed and wait upon him (उपाध्वं (*upādhvaṃ*) = उपतिष्ठत (*upatiṣṭhata*)). Having come, this Vasiṣṭha, as foremost priest and Brahmā at the sacrifice, supports the उक्थभृतं (*ukthabhṛtaṃ*), the one who carries the recitations (śastras); he supports the सामभृतं (*sāmabhṛtaṃ*), the Udgātṛ; and, bearing the pressing-stone, he supports the Adhvaryu who also carries it. प्र वदाति (*pra vadāti*): whatever is to be spoken or done at the sacrifice on an occasion such as a blunder (भ्रेष (*bhreṣa*)), that too he declares. Thus Indra speaks to the Tṛtsus.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- प्रतृदः (*pratṛdaḥ*)—O Tṛtsus
- वः (*vaḥ*)—to you
- वसिष्ठः (*vasiṣṭhaḥ*)—Vasiṣṭha
- आ गच्छाति (*ā gacchāti*)—comes
- एनं (*enaṃ*)—this Vasiṣṭha
- सुमनस्यमानाः (*sumanasyamānāḥ*)—honouring with faith
- उप आध्वं (*upa ādhvaṃ*)—go to meet
- (this Vasiṣṭha) अग्रे (*agre*)—being the priest at the sacrifice
- उक्थभृतं (*ukthabhṛtaṃ*)—is the leader of the Hotṛ who recites the śastra
- सामभृतं (*sāmabhṛtaṃ*)—guide also to the Udgātṛ
- बिभर्ति (*bibharti*)—supports
- ग्रावाणं (*grāvāṇaṃ*)—the pressing-stone
- बिभ्रत् (*bibhrat*)—he supports the Adhvaryu who holds it
- प्र वदाति (*pra vadāti*)—teaches the rites to be performed at the sacrifice

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Tṛtsus, the venerable Vasiṣṭha comes to you. Go to meet him, honouring him with faith. This Vasiṣṭha, being the chief priest at the sacrifice, is guide and leader to the Hotṛ who recites the śastra and to the Udgātṛ who chants the sāman. He is also a helper to the Adhvaryu who holds the pressing-stone. He teaches all the rites to be performed at the sacrifice.

##### English translation
Tritsus, Agastya comes to you ; welcome him with devoted minds,
**NOTE**

### Printed p. 148
and he in the foremost station directs the reciter of the prayer, the chaunter of the hymn, the grinder of the stone, and repeats (what is to be repeated).

## Sūkta 34 (Maṇḍala 7)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> तृतीयोऽनुवाके द्वाविंशति सूक्तानि । तत्र प्र शुक्रेति पंचविंशत्यृचं प्रथमं सूक्तं । अत्रेयमनुक्रमणिका । प्र शुक्रा पंचाधिका वैश्वदेवं हाद्या एकविंशतिर्द्विपदा अष्टा महेरर्धर्च उत्तरोऽहिर्बुध्न्ययेति । वसिष्ठ ऋषिः । आद्या एकविंशतिर्द्विपदा विंशत्यक्षरा विराजो द्वाविंशाद्याश्चतस्रस्त्रिष्टुभः । विश्वे देवा देवता ॥ पृष्ठ्ये दशरात्रे चतुर्थेऽहनि वैश्वदेवशस्त्रे इदं सूक्तं वैश्वदेवनिविद्धानं । सूत्रितं च । प्र शुक्रैत्विति वैश्वदेवं । आ. ८-८ । इति ॥ षोडशिन्या धूर्ष्वस्मा इति द्विपदा । आ. ६-२ । इति ॥ महाव्रतेऽप्येषा द्विपदा । तथैव पंचमारण्यके सूत्रितं । आ धूर्ष्वस्मा इत्येका सूददोहाः । ऐ. आ. ५-१ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In the third anuvāka there are twenty-two sūktas. Of these the first is the sūkta of twenty-five ṛks beginning प्र शुक्रा (*pra śukrā*). Sāyaṇa quotes the Anukramaṇikā for it: प्र शुक्रा पंचाधिका वैश्वदेवं हाद्या एकविंशतिर्द्विपदा अष्टा महेरर्धर्च उत्तरोऽहिर्बुध्न्ययेति (*pra śukrā paṃcādhikā vaiśvadevaṃ hādyā ekaviṃśatirdvipadā aṣṭā maherardharca uttaro 'hirbudhnyayeti*). The seer is Vasiṣṭha. The first twenty-one ṛks are dvipadā of twenty syllables, of the virāj type; from the twenty-second, four ṛks are triṣṭubh. The deity is the Viśve Devāḥ. At the Pṛṣṭhya Daśarātra, on the fourth day, this sūkta serves as the Vaiśvadeva-nividdhāna in the Vaiśvadeva śastra. It is enjoined by the sūtra: प्र शुक्रैत्विति वैश्वदेवं (*pra śukraitviti vaiśvadevaṃ*) (Ā. 8-8). Its use in the Ṣoḍaśin is given by षोडशिन्या धूर्ष्वस्मा इति द्विपदा (*ṣoḍaśinyā dhūrṣvasmā iti dvipadā*) (Ā. 6-2). This dvipadā is also used in the Mahāvrata, and is enjoined likewise in the fifth Āraṇyaka: आ धूर्ष्वस्मा इत्येका सूददोहाः (*ā dhūrṣvasmā ityekā sūdadohāḥ*) (Ai. Ā. 5-1).

##### Anuvāda

In the third anuvāka of the seventh maṇḍala of the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā there are twenty-two sūktas. Of them the sūkta प्र शुक्रा (*pra śukrā*) is the first. It has twenty-five ṛks. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: प्र शुक्रा पंचाधिका वैश्वदेवं हाद्या एकविंशतिर्द्विपदा अष्टा महेरर्धर्च उत्तरोऽहिर्बुध्न्ययेति (*pra śukrā paṃcādhikā vaiśvadevaṃ hādyā ekaviṃśatirdvipadā aṣṭā maherardharca uttaro 'hirbudhnyayeti*). The seer of this sūkta is Vasiṣṭha; the deity is the Viśve Devāḥ. The first twenty-one ṛks of this sūkta are in the metre called dvipadā virāj, of twenty syllables. The four ṛks from the twenty-second onward are in the triṣṭubh metre. It is explained by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, in the sūtra प्र शुक्रैत्विति वैश्वदेवं (*pra śukraitviti vaiśvadevaṃ*) (Ā. 8-8), that in the Vaiśvadeva śastra mantras recited on the fourth day of the Daśarātra sacrifice called Pṛṣṭhya, this sūkta is to be recited as the Vaiśvadeva-nividdhāna sūkta. That it has a use in the sacrifice called Ṣoḍaśī is explained by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra in the sūtra आ धूर्ष्वस्मा इति द्विपदा (*ā dhūrṣvasmā iti dvipadā*) (Ā. 6-2). That the ṛks of this sūkta in the dvipadā metre have a use also in the Mahāvrata-iṣṭi is indicated by the Aitareya Āraṇyaka, in the fifth Āraṇyaka, in the sentence आ धूर्ष्वस्मा इत्येका सूददोहाः (*ā dhūrṣvasmā ityekā sūdadohāḥ*) (Ai. 5-2-3). **NOTE**

##### Particulars

- Sūkta—34
- Maṇḍala—7; Anuvāka—3; Sūkta—34
- Aṣṭaka—5; Adhyāya—3; Varga—21, 22, 23
- Ṛks in the sūkta—25
- Ṛṣi—वसिष्ठः (*vasiṣṭhaḥ*)
- Devatā—1-14, 19-25: विश्वे देवाः (*viśve devāḥ*); 15: अहिः (*ahiḥ*); 17: अहिर्बुध्न्यः (*ahirbudhnyaḥ*)
- Chandas—1-21: द्विपदा (*dvipadā*); 22-25: त्रिष्टुप् (*triṣṭup*)

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र शु॒क्रैतु॑ दे॒वी म॑नी॒षा अ॒स्मत् सुत॑ष्टो॒ रथो॒ न वा॒जी ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 149

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । शुक्रा । एतु । देवी । मनीषा । अस्मत् । सुतष्टः । रथः । न । वाजी ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> शुक्रा दीप्ता देवी सर्वेषां कामानां प्रदात्री मनीषा स्तुतिरस्मदस्क्रोऽस्मिन् सूक्ते स्तोप्यमाणान्देवान् वाजी वेगवान् सुतष्टः सुसंस्कृतो रथो न रथ इव प्रैतु । प्रगच्छतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

शुक्रा (*śukrā*) is bright; देवी (*devī*) is the giver of all desires; मनीषा (*manīṣā*) is the praise. Let this praise of ours go forth to the gods who are to be praised in this sūkta, like a swift (वाजी (*vājī*)), well-fashioned (सुतष्टः (*sutaṣṭaḥ*)) chariot.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- शुक्रा (*śukrā*)—possessed of splendour
- देवी (*devī*)—and granting desires
- मनीषा (*manīṣā*)—our praise
- अस्मत् (*asmat*)—recited by us
- वाजी (*vājī*)—swift-going
- सुतष्टः (*sutaṣṭaḥ*)—and well-fashioned
- रथो न (*ratho na*)—like a chariot
- प्र एतु (*pra etu*)—let it speed (to the gods)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May our praise, full of splendour and granting desires, recited by us, speed like a swift and well-fashioned chariot and go and reach the gods.

##### English translation
May pure and divine praise proceed from us (to the gods) like a swift, well-constructed chariot.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वि॒दुः पृ॑थि॒व्या दि॒वो ज॒नित्रं॑ शृ॒ण्वंत्यापो॒ अध॒ क्षरं॑तीः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> विदुः । पृथिव्याः । दिवः । जनित्रं । शृण्वंति । आपः । अध । क्षरंतीः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अस्यामृच्यापः स्तूयंते । क्षरंतीः क्षरंत्य आपो दिवः पृथिव्याश्च । उभयोरपि लोकयोरित्यर्थः । जनित्रमुत्पत्तिं विदुः । जानंति । अथापि च शृण्वंति स्तोत्राणीति शेषः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In this ṛk the waters are praised. The flowing waters (क्षरंतीः (*kṣaraṃtīḥ*)) know the जनित्रं (*janitraṃ*), the origin, of heaven and of earth, that is, of both worlds. And they also hear; "the praises" is to be supplied.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- क्षरंतीः (*kṣaraṃtīḥ*)—flowing
- आपः (*āpaḥ*)—the waters
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—of the heavenly world (and)
- पृथिव्याः (*pṛthivyāḥ*)—of the earth
- जनित्रं (*janitraṃ*)—the manner of origin
- विदुः (*viduḥ*)—have known
- अध (*adha*)—therefore
- शृण्वंति (*śṛṇvaṃti*)—they hear our praises

### Printed p. 150

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The flowing waters have known the manner of origin of the heavenly world and of the earth. Therefore they hear our praises.

##### English translation
The flowing waters have known the origin of earth and heaven : may they now hear (our praises).

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आप॑श्चिदस्मै॒ पिन्वं॑त पृ॒थ्वीर्वृ॒त्रेषु॒ शूरा॒ मंसं॑त उ॒ग्राः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आपः । चित् । अस्मै । पिन्वंत । पृथ्वीः । वृत्रेषु । शूराः । मंसंते । उग्राः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इंद्रोऽस्मिन्दृचे स्तूयते । पृथ्वीः पृथ्व्यः प्रथमाना आपश्चिदापोऽप्यस्मा इंद्रायै पिन्वंत । प्याययंते । वृत्रेषूपद्रवेषु सत्सूग्रा उद्गूर्णास्तेजस्विनो वा शूरा योद्धारोऽपि मंसंते । इममेवेंद्रं स्तुवंति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In this ṛk Indra is praised. The पृथ्वीः (*pṛthvīḥ*), the broad, extending waters also swell, that is, nourish, this Indra. When there are troubles (वृत्रेषु (*vṛtreṣu*)), the उग्राः (*ugrāḥ*), mighty or brilliant, शूराः (*śūrāḥ*), warriors, also मंसंते (*maṃsaṃte*): they praise this very Indra.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पृथ्वीः (*pṛthvīḥ*)—spread out widely
- आपश्चित् (*āpaścit*)—the waters too
- अस्मै (*asmai*)—to this Indra
- पिन्वंत (*pinvaṃta*)—give nourishment
- वृत्रेषु (*vṛtreṣu*)—in battles that destroy enemies
- उग्राः (*ugrāḥ*)—showing terrible strength
- शूराः (*śūrāḥ*)—the valiant also
- मंसंते (*maṃsaṃte*)—praise this Indra

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The waters, spread out widely, are also nourishing to this Indra. The valiant, showing terrible strength in battles that destroy enemies, also praise this Indra.

##### English translation
The vast waters offer nurishment to Indra : fierce warriors, (combating) with foes, glorify him.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ धू॒र्ष्व॑स्मै॒ दधा॒ताश्वा॒निंद्रो॒ न व॒ज्री हिर॑ण्यबाहुः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । धूःऽसु । अस्मै । दधात । अश्वान् । इंद्रः । न । वज्री । हिरण्यबाहुः ॥ ४ ॥

### Printed p. 151

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अस्मै । षष्ठ्यर्थे चतुर्थी । अस्येंद्रस्यागमनायाश्वान् धूर्षु रथस्या दधात । इंद्रो न । नेति चार्थे । इंद्रो वज्री वज्रवान् हिरण्यबाहुर्हिरण्यहस्तश्च भवति ॥ षोडशिनि शस्यमानत्वादस्या ऐंद्रत्वं गम्यते । आ धूर्ष्वस्मा इत्येत्रास्मा इत्यस्य पदस्यानुदात्तत्वं पूर्वस्यामिंद्रस्य प्रकृतत्वात् अत एव पूर्वास्यैंद्रीति विज्ञायते । पूर्वस्यामुस्मै चैस्मा इत्येत्रानुदात्तत्वमल्पीयोऽर्थतरमनुदात्तमिति । तथा च यास्कः । अस्येति चोदात्तं प्रथमादेशेऽनुदात्तमन्यादेशे तीव्रार्थतरमनुदात्तमल्पीयोऽर्थतरमनुदात्तं । नि. ४-२५ । इति । अल्पीयस्त्वं चेदंशब्दप्रवृत्तिनिमित्तस्य संनिधानस्य दूरस्थत्वेनेत्यवगंतव्यं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

अस्मै (*asmai*) is a dative used in the sense of the genitive. Horses are to be yoked to the yoke-poles of the chariot for the coming of this Indra. Indra is the wielder of the thunderbolt and golden-armed. Because this Ṛk is recited in the Ṣoḍaśin, its Indra-deity is understood. In आ धूर्ष्वस्मा (*ā dhūrṣvasmā*) the word अस्मै (*asmai*) is unaccented, because Indra was the subject of the preceding Ṛk; hence the preceding Ṛk is known to be addressed to Indra. Sāyaṇa quotes Yāska (Ni. 4-25): अस्येति चोदात्तं प्रथमादेशेऽनुदात्तमन्यादेशे तीव्रार्थतरमनुदात्तमल्पीयोऽर्थतरमनुदात्तं । न (*asyeti codāttaṃ prathamādeśe 'nudāttamanyādeśe tīvrārthataramanudāttamalpīyo 'rthataramanudāttaṃ | na*) The "lesser" sense is to be understood from the remoteness of the proximity that is the cause of the use of the word *idaṃ*.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रो न (*iṃdro na*)—this Indra also
- वज्री (*vajrī*)—the bearer of the thunderbolt weapon
- हिरण्यबाहुः (*hiraṇyabāhuḥ*)—one with golden arms
- अस्मै (*asmai*)—for the coming of this Indra
- धूर्षु (*dhūrṣu*)—in the yoke-poles of the chariot
- अश्वान् (*aśvān*)—the horses
- आ दधात (*ā dadhāta*)—join and yoke

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

This Indra also is the bearer of the thunderbolt weapon and has golden arms. For the coming of this Indra, yoke the horses to the yoke-poles of the chariot.

##### English translation
Yoke for him the horses of his chariot, for Indra is the wielder of the thunderbolt, the golden-armed.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒भि प्र स्था॒ताहे॑व य॒ज्ञं याते॑व॒ पत्म॒न्त्मना॑ हिनोते ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अभि । प्र । स्थात । अहऽइव । यज्ञं । याताऽइव । पत्मन् । त्मना । हिनोत ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यज्ञस्तुतिरियं । हे जनाः यज्ञमभि प्र स्थात । अभिक्रमत । अहेवेति पूरणौ । अपि च पत्मन् पत्मनि यज्ञमार्गे त्मना स्वयमेव यातेव गंतेव हिनोते गच्छत ॥ हि गताविति धातुः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This is a praise of the sacrifice. "O people, set out towards the sacrifice, advance." अहेव (*aheva*) is an expletive (pūraṇa). Further, on the path (पत्मन् (*patman*)) of the sacrifice go of your own accord (त्मना (*tmanā*)), as one who goes (यातेव (*yāteva*)). हिनोते (*hinote*) means "go"; the root is हि (*hi*) in the sense of going.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यज्ञं अभि (*yajñaṃ abhi*)—towards the sacrifice (O sacrificers and the rest)
- प्र स्थात (*pra sthāta*)—set out
- पत्मन् (*patman*)—on the path of the sacrifice
- त्मना (*tmanā*)—by one's own impulse
- यातेव (*yāteva*)—like a traveller
- हिनोते (*hinote*)—go

### Printed p. 152

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O sacrificers and the rest, set out towards the sacrifice. As a traveller on the road goes by his own impulse, without the constraint of others, so set out following the path of the sacrifice.

##### English translation
Proceed to the sacrifice like one who goes along the road ; proceed of your own accord.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्मना॑ स॒मत्सु॑ हि॒नोत॑ य॒ज्ञं दधा॑त के॒तुं जना॑य वी॒रं ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> त्मना । समत्सु । हिनोत । यज्ञं । दधात । केतुं । जनाय । वीरं ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उक्तस्यैव विवरणमत्र । हे मदीया जनाः समत्सु संग्रामेषु त्मना स्वयमेव हिनोत । गच्छत । अपि च केतुं प्रज्ञापकं वीरं पापानां वारयितारं । नाशकमित्यर्थः । यज्ञं जनाय लोकाय । तद्रक्षणार्थमित्यर्थः । दधात । विधत्त ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This is an elaboration of what was said. "O people who are mine, go of your own accord (त्मना (*tmanā*)) into the battles (समत्सु (*samatsu*))." Further, perform (दधात (*dadhāta*), "ordain") for the people, that is, for their protection, the sacrifice, which is केतुं (*ketuṃ*), the making known, and वीरं (*vīraṃ*), the warder-off, that is the destroyer, of sins.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- समत्सु (*samatsu*)—to the battles (O sacrificers and the rest)
- त्मना (*tmanā*)—of your own impulse
- हिनोत (*hinota*)—go
- केतुं (*ketuṃ*)—that which makes known
- वीरं (*vīraṃ*)—that which destroys sin
- यज्ञं (*yajñaṃ*)—the sacrifice
- जनाय (*janāya*)—for the good of the world
- दधात (*dadhāta*)—perform

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O sacrificers and the rest, go of your own impulse to the battles. Perform the sacrifice, which makes known and destroys sin, for the good of the world.

##### English translation
Go of your own accord to battle : celebrate the significant and expiatory sacrifice for (the good of) mankind.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उद॑स्य॒ शुष्मा॑द्भा॒नुर्नार्त॒ बिभ॑र्ति भा॒रं पृ॑थि॒वी न भूम॑ ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उत् । अस्य । शुष्मात् । भानुः । न । आर्त । बिभर्ति । भारं । पृथिवी । न । भूम ॥ ७ ॥

### Printed p. 153

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अस्य यज्ञस्य शुष्माद्बलाद्भानुः सूर्य उदार्त । उद्गच्छति । भूम भूतानि पृथिवीव भारं लोकस्यायं यज्ञो बिभर्ति च ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

From the strength (शुष्मात् (*śuṣmāt*)) of this sacrifice the sun (भानुः (*bhānuḥ*)) rises (उदार्त (*udārta*): goes up). This sacrifice also bears the burden of the world, as the earth bears (all) beings (भूम (*bhūma*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अस्य (*asya*)—of this sacrifice
- शुष्मात् (*śuṣmāt*)—by the strength
- भानुः (*bhānuḥ*)—the sun
- उदार्त (*udārta*)—rises and comes
- भूम (*bhūma*)—all beings
- पृथिवी न (*pṛthivī na*)—as the earth bears
- भारं (*bhāraṃ*)—the burden of this world
- बिभर्ति (*bibharti*)—this sacrifice bears

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

By the power of this sacrifice the sun rises. As the earth bears all beings, this sacrifice bears the burden of this world.

##### English translation
From the force of this (sacrifice) the sun rises : it sustains the burden (of the world) as (earth) supports many (beings).

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ह्वया॑मि दे॒वा अया॑तुरग्ने॒ साध॑न्नृ॒तेन॒ धियं॑ दधामि ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ह्वयामि । देवान् । अयातुः । अग्ने । साधन् । ऋतेन । धियं । दधामि ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अस्मिन्नृचे देवाः स्तूयंते । हे अग्ने अयातुरहिंसादिनियमयुक्तेनर्तेन यज्ञेन साधन्कामान्साधयन् देवान् ह्वयामि । अपि च धियं देवानां परिचरणात्मकं कर्म दधामि । करोमीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In this Ṛk the gods are praised. "O Agni, accomplishing (साधन् (*sādhan*)) desires by the sacrifice (ऋतेन (*ṛtena*)) that is endowed with the rule of non-injury and the like (अयातुः (*ayātuḥ*)), I invoke the gods. Further, I perform (दधामि (*dadhāmi*): I do) धियं (*dhiyaṃ*), the work consisting of serving the gods."

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni
- अयातुः (*ayātuḥ*)—endowed with the rule of non-injury and the like
- ऋतेन (*ṛtena*)—by the sacrifice
- साधन् (*sādhan*)—worshipping
- देवान् (*devān*)—the gods
- ह्वयामि (*hvayāmi*)—I pray and call
- धियं (*dhiyaṃ*)—the work of worship of the gods
- दधामि (*dadhāmi*)—I accomplish

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, worshipping with a sacrifice endowed with the rule of non-injury and the like, I pray to and call the gods. I accomplish the work of worship of the gods.

##### English translation
I invoke the gods, Agni, propitiating them by an inoffensive rite, I celebrate a pious act.

### Printed p. 154

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒भि वो॑ दे॒वीं धियं॑ दधिध्वं॒ प्र वो॑ देव॒त्रा वाचं॑ कृणुध्वं

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अभि । वः । देवीं । धियं । दधिध्वं । प्र । वः । देवत्रा । वाचं । कृणुध्वं ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे जनाः वो यूयमभि देवानुद्दिश्य देवीं दीप्तां धियं कर्म दधिध्वं । विधत्त । अपि च वो यूयं देवत्रा देवेषु वाचं स्तुतिरूपां प्र कृणुध्वं । प्रकर्षेण कुरुध्वं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O people, you, directing yourselves to the gods, perform (दधिध्वं (*dadhidhvaṃ*): ordain) the shining (देवीं (*devīṃ*)) work (धियं (*dhiyaṃ*)). Further, you, among the gods (देवत्रा (*devatrā*)), utter (प्र कृणुध्वं (*pra kṛṇudhvaṃ*): make forth, that is, make excellently) speech (वाचं (*vācaṃ*)) in the form of praise."

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वः (*vaḥ*)—you (O sacrificers and the rest)
- अभि (*abhi*)—directed to the gods
- देवीं (*devīṃ*)—shining
- धियं (*dhiyaṃ*)—the work
- दधिध्वं (*dadhidhvaṃ*)—accomplish
- वः (*vaḥ*)—you
- देवत्रा (*devatrā*)—in the presence of the gods
- वाचं (*vācaṃ*)—speech in the form of praise
- प्र कृणुध्वं (*pra kṛṇudhvaṃ*)—recite abundantly

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O sacrificers and the rest, directing yourselves to the gods, accomplish the shining work. In the presence of the gods recite in the best manner speech in the form of praise.

##### English translation
Offer, (worshippers), your heavenly worship : earnestly address your praises to the gods.

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ च॑ष्ट आसां॒ पाथो॑ न॒दीनां॒ वरु॑ण उ॒ग्रः स॒हस्र॑चक्षाः ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । चष्टे । आसां । पाथः । नदीनां । वरुणः । उग्रः । सहस्रचक्षाः ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सहस्रचक्षा बहुचक्षुर्वरुण आसां नदीनां पाथो जलमा चष्टे । अभिपश्यति । कीदृशो वरुणः । उग्र उद्गूर्ण ओजस्वी वा ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Varuṇa, thousand-eyed (many-eyed), looks upon (आ चष्टे (*ā caṣṭe*): surveys) the water (पाथः (*pāthaḥ*)) of these rivers. What is Varuṇa like? He is उग्र (*ugra*): mighty, or full of vigour.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उग्रः (*ugraḥ*)—one of exceeding strength
- सहस्रचक्षाः (*sahasracakṣāḥ*)—one with many eyes
- वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*)—Varuṇa
- आसां नदीनां (*āsāṃ nadīnāṃ*)—of these flowing rivers
- पाथः (*pāthaḥ*)—the water
- आ चष्टे (*ā caṣṭe*)—looks upon

### Printed p. 155

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Varuṇa, of exceeding strength and with many eyes, looks upon the water of these flowing rivers.

##### English translation
The fierce Varuna, the thousand eyed, contemplates the water of these rivers.

#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> राजा॑ रा॒ष्ट्रानां॒ पेशो॑ न॒दीना॒मनु॑त्तमस्मै क्ष॒त्रं वि॒श्वायु॑ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> राजा । राष्ट्रानां । पेशः । नदीनां । अनुत्तं । अस्मै । क्षत्रं । विश्वआयु ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> राष्ट्रानां राष्ट्राणां ॥ णत्वाभावश्छांदसः । ईश्वराणामपि वरुणो राजेश्वरो
> भवति । नदीनां पेशो रूपं रूपकृदसि भवतीत्यर्थः । अस्मै । षष्ठ्यर्थे चतुर्थी ।
> अस्य वरुणस्य क्षत्रं बलमनुत्तमन्यैरबाधितं विश्वायु सर्वतो गंतृ भवतीति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

राष्ट्रानां (*rāṣṭrānāṃ*) stands for राष्ट्राणां (*rāṣṭrāṇāṃ*); the absence of the change to ṇa is Vedic usage. Varuṇa is the king, the lord, even of lords. पेशः (*peśaḥ*) is the form of the rivers: he is the maker of their form. अस्मै (*asmai*) is a dative in the sense of the genitive: of this Varuṇa the क्षत्रं (*kṣatraṃ*), strength, is अनुत्तं (*anuttaṃ*), unimpeded by others, and विश्वायु (*viśvāyu*), going everywhere.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*)—the god Varuṇa
- राष्ट्रानां (*rāṣṭrānāṃ*)—of all rulers of realms
- राजा (*rājā*)—is the lord
- नदीनां (*nadīnāṃ*)—for the rivers
- पेशः (*peśaḥ*)—is the giver of an attractive form
- अस्मै (*asmai*)—of this Varuṇa
- क्षत्रं (*kṣatraṃ*)—the strength
- अनुत्तमं (*anuttamaṃ*)—is without a second
- विश्वायु (*viśvāyu*)—is all-pervading

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

This god Varuṇa is the lord of all rulers of realms. He is the giver of an attractive form to the rivers. The strength of this Varuṇa is without a second and all-pervading.

##### English translation
He is the king of kings : the beauty of the rivers : his all-pervading strength is irresistible.

#### Ṛk 12

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अवि॑ष्टो अ॒स्मान्विश्वा॑सु वि॒क्ष्वद्युं॑ कृणोत॒ु शंसं॑ निनि॒त्सोः ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अविष्टो इति । अस्मान् । विश्वासु । विक्षु । अद्युं । कृणोतु । शंसं । निनित्सोः ॥

### Printed p. 156

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आयं वैश्वानरो दैवः । हे देवाः अस्मान् विश्वासु सर्वासु विशु प्रजासु ।
> अविष्ट उ इति समुदितमविष्टो इति । अविष्ट । रक्षत । शंसं निनित्सोर्निंदितुमिच्छते
> शत्रोरद्युमदीप्तिं कृणोत । कुरुत च ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This (Ṛk) is addressed to Vaiśvānara, a divine being. "O gods, protect (अविष्ट (*aviṣṭa*)) us among all (विश्वासु (*viśvāsu*)) peoples (विक्षु (*vikṣu*)). अविष्ट उ (*aviṣṭa u*) combined is अविष्टो (*aviṣṭo*)." Make (कृणोत (*kṛṇota*)) the शंसं (*śaṃsaṃ*) of the enemy who wishes to revile (निनित्सोः (*ninitsoḥ*)) अद्युं (*adyuṃ*), without lustre; and do so.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अस्मान् (*asmān*)—us (O gods)
- विश्वासु (*viśvāsu*)—all
- विशु (*viśu*)—among the peoples
- अविष्ट (*aviṣṭa*)—protect
- निनित्सोः (*ninitsoḥ*)—of the enemy who is a reviler
- शंसं (*śaṃsaṃ*)—the evil intent
- आद्युमं (*ādyumaṃ*)—as without power
- कृणोत (*kṛṇota*)—make

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O gods, protect us among all the peoples. Make the evil intent of the reviling enemy powerless.

##### English translation
Protect us, gods, among all people ; render extinct the calumny of the malevolent.

#### Ṛk 13

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> व्ये॑तु दि॒द्युद्द्वि॒षामशे॑वा यु॒योत॒ विष्व॒ग्रप॑स्त॒नूनां॑ ॥ १३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वि । एतु । दिद्युत् । द्विषां । अशेवा । युयोत । विष्वक् । रपः । तनूनां ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> द्विषां शत्रूणां दिद्युदायुधमशेवासुखकरी विष्वक् सर्वतो व्येतु । अपगच्छतु । तनूनामंगानां रपः पापं देवा युयोत । अस्मत्तः पृथक्कुरुत ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Let the दिद्युत् (*didyut*), the weapon, of the foes (द्विषां (*dviṣāṃ*): enemies), unauspicious (अशेवा (*aśevā*): giving no comfort), go away (व्येतु (*vyetu*)) on every side (विष्वक् (*viṣvak*)). O gods, युयोत (*yuyota*): separate from us रपः (*rapaḥ*), the sin of our bodies (तनूनां (*tanūnāṃ*): limbs).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- द्विषां (*dviṣāṃ*)—of our enemies
- अशेवा (*aśevā*)—inauspicious
- दिद्युत् (*didyut*)—weapons
- विष्वक् (*viṣvak*)—everywhere
- व्येतु (*vyetu*)—let them move far away (O gods)
- तनूनां (*tanūnāṃ*)—of our bodies
- रपः (*rapaḥ*)—the sin
- युयोत (*yuyota*)—separate from us

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O gods, let the inauspicious weapons of our enemies move far away from us in every direction. Separate from us the sin committed by our bodies.

##### English translation
May the blazing (weapons) of foes pass by innocuous ; separate, (gods) universally (from us) the sin of our bodies.

### Printed p. 157

#### Ṛk 14

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अवी॑न्नो अ॒ग्निर्ह॒व्यान्नमो॑भिः॒ प्रेष्ठो॑ अस्मा अधायि॒ स्तोमः॑

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अवीत् । नः । अग्निः । हव्यऽअत् । नमःऽभिः । प्रेष्ठः । अस्मै । अधायि । स्तोमः ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हव्याद्धव्यानामत्ता अग्निर्नमोभिरस्मदीयैर्नमस्कारैः प्रेष्ठः प्रियतमः सन्नोऽस्मानवीत् । रक्षतु । अस्मा अग्नये स्तोमः स्तोत्रमधायि । अस्माभिर्व्यधायि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Agni is हव्यात् (*havyāt*), the eater of oblations. Being most dear through our homage (नमोभिः (*namobhiḥ*), our salutations), he is to protect us (अवीत् (*avīt*), "let him protect"). To him this praise (स्तोमः (*stomaḥ*), hymn) has been offered by us.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- हव्यात् (*havyāt*)—the one who eats the oblation
- अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—Agni
- नमोभिः (*namobhiḥ*)—satisfied by our praises
- प्रेष्ठः (*preṣṭhaḥ*)—being the most dear to us
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- अवीत् (*avīt*)—let him protect
- अस्मै (*asmai*)—addressed to this Agni
- स्तोमः (*stomaḥ*)—the hymn
- अधायि (*adhāyi*)—is offered by us

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May Agni, the eater of oblations, satisfied by our praises and being most dear to us, protect us. The hymn addressed to this Agni is offered by us.

##### English translation
May Agni, the feeder on oblations, propitiated by our homage, protect us ; to him has our praise been addressed.

#### Ṛk 15

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स॒जूर्दे॒वेभि॑र॒पां नपा॑तं॒ सखा॑यं कृध्वं शि॒वो नो॑ अस्तु ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सऽजूः । देवेभिः । अपां । नपातं । सखायं । कृध्वं । शिवः । नः । अस्तु ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे स्तोतः अपामुदकानां नपातं पुत्रमग्निं । सूनुर्नपादित्यपत्यनामसु पाठात् । देवेभिर्देवैः सजूः सह सखायं मित्रं स्तुतिभिः कृध्वं । कुरुध्वं । स चापां नपान्नोऽस्मभ्यं शिवः सुखकरोऽस्तु । भवतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O praiser, with praises make Agni, the son (नपातं (*napātaṃ*)) of the waters, a friend, together with the gods." *napāt* is read among the words for offspring: *sūnurnapāt*. May that Agni, the son of the waters, be auspicious, a bringer of happiness, to us.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O praisers,) सखायं (*sakhāyaṃ*)—Agni, who is a friend to us
- अपां (*apāṃ*)—of the waters
- नपातं (*napātaṃ*)—who is as a son; him
- देवेभिः सजूः (*devebhiḥ sajūḥ*)—together with the other gods
- कृध्वं (*kṛdhvaṃ*)—worship
- (may that same Agni) नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- शिवः (*śivaḥ*)—be a bringer of good
- अस्तु (*astu*)—let him be

### Printed p. 158

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O praisers, worship together with the other gods Agni, who is a friend to us and as a son to the waters. May that same Agni be a bringer of good to us.

##### English translation
Glorify along with the gods our friend, the grandson of the waters : may he be propitious to us.

#### Ṛk 16

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒ब्जामु॒क्थैरहिं॑ गृणीषे बु॒ध्ने न॒दीनां॒ रजः॑सु॒ षीद॑न् ॥ १६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अप्ऽजां । उक्थैः । अहिं । गृणीषे । बुध्ने । नदीनां । रजःसु । सीदन् ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अहिं मेघानामाहंतारं नदीनामुदकानां बुध्ने स्थाने । बुध्नमंतरिक्षं बद्धा अस्मिन् धृता आप इति व्युत्पत्तेः । तस्मिन्रजःसूदकेषु सीदन् सीदंतमब्जामप्सु जातमिममग्निमुक्थैः स्तोत्रैर्गृणीषे । बुध्ने नदीनां रजःसूदकेषु सीदन् बुध्नमंतरिक्षं बद्धा अस्मिन् धृता आप इति । नि. १०-४४ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

अहिं (*ahiṃ*) is the smiter of the clouds. बुध्ने (*budhne*) means in the place of the rivers (waters), the atmosphere, from the derivation "the waters are bound and held in it". You praise with hymns (उक्थैः (*ukthaiḥ*)) this Agni, water-born (अब्जां (*abjāṃ*)), sitting in the waters of that region. He cites बुध्नमंतरिक्षं बद्धा अस्मिन् धृता आप इति (*budhnamaṃtarikṣaṃ baddhā asmin dhṛtā āpa iti*) (Ni. 10-44).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अहिं (*ahiṃ*)—the one who splits the clouds
- नदीनां (*nadīnāṃ*)—of the waters
- बुध्ने (*budhne*)—in the atmosphere, which is the place
- रजःसु (*rajaḥsu*)—in the waters
- सीदन् (*sīdan*)—the one who abides
- अब्जां (*abjāṃ*)—Agni, born in the waters
- उक्थैः (*ukthaiḥ*)—with hymns
- गृणीषे (*gṛṇīṣe*)—I praise

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

I praise with hymns Agni, who splits the clouds, who abides in the waters in the atmosphere, the place of the waters, and who is born in the waters.

##### English translation
I glorify with hymns the disperser of the clouds in the firmament : the water-born, sitting amongst the waters of the rivers.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

बुध्ने (*budhne*)—Yāska gives two meanings of the word बुध्न (*budhna*):
बुध्नमंतरिक्षं बद्धा अस्मिन् धृता आप इति वा । इदमपीतरद्बुध्नमेतस्मादेव । बद्धा अस्मिन् धृताः प्राणा इति ॥ (*budhnamaṃtarikṣaṃ baddhā asmin dhṛtā āpa iti vā | idamapītaradbudhnametasmādeva | baddhā asmin dhṛtāḥ prāṇā iti ‖*) (Ni. 10-44)

### Printed p. 159
बुध्न (*budhna*) means the atmosphere, because the waters in the form of clouds are held back and made to stand in this atmosphere. Or: in this atmosphere the waters are held (in the form of vapour or of clouds). Therefore बुध्न (*budhna*) means the atmosphere.
There is another meaning of बुध्न (*budhna*), the body, because the vital breaths are held in the body; that is, the body, which gives the breaths room to dwell, is called बुध्न (*budhna*).

#### Ṛk 17

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> मा नोऽहि॑र्बु॒ध्न्यो॑ रि॒षे धा॒न्मा य॒ज्ञो अ॑स्य स्रिधदृता॒योः॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> मा । नः । अहिः । बुध्न्यः । रिषे । धात् । मा । यज्ञः । अस्य । स्रिधत् । ऋतऽयोः॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अहिर्बुध्न्यः । बुध्नेऽंतरिक्षे भवो बुध्न्यः । अहिश्चासौ बुध्न्यश्चेत्यहिर्बुध्न्योऽग्निर्नोऽस्मान्रिषे हिंसकाय मा धात् । मा ददातु । अस्य ऋतायोर्यज्ञकामस्य यजमानस्य यज्ञो मा च स्रिधत् । न क्षीयेत । यद्वा । अस्याहिर्बुध्न्यस्येममहिर्बुध्न्यमुद्दिश्य ऋतायोर्यज्ञकामस्य यो यज्ञः स न क्षीयेतेत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

अहिर्बुध्न्यः (*ahirbudhnyaḥ*) is the one who is in the atmosphere (बुध्ने (*budhne*)), and is also अहि (*ahi*): Agni so named. Let him not give us over to the one who harms (रिषे (*riṣe*)). And let the sacrifice of this sacrifice-desiring (*ṛtāyoḥ*) worshipper not fail (स्रिधत् (*sridhat*), be destroyed). Or else: the sacrifice of one desiring sacrifice, offered to this Ahirbudhnya, is not to be destroyed.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अहिर्बुध्न्यः (*ahirbudhnyaḥ*)—Agni named Ahirbudhnya
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- रिषे (*riṣe*)—to harm
- मा धात् (*mā dhāt*)—let him not have the wish
- अस्य (*asya*)—of this
- ऋतयोः (*ṛtayoḥ*)—worshipper
- यज्ञः (*yajñaḥ*)—the power of the sacrifice
- मा स्रिधत् (*mā sridhat*)—let it not fail

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May Agni named Ahirbudhnya not have the wish to harm us. May the power of this worshipper's sacrifice not fail.

##### English translation
Let not Ahirbudhnya be disposed to work us harm : let not the sacrifice of the worshipper be disregarded.

#### Ṛk 18

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒त न॑ ए॒षु नृषु॒ श्रवो॑ धुः॒ प्र रा॒ये यं॑तु॒ शर्धं॑तो अ॒र्यः

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उत । नः । एषु । नृषु । श्रवः । धुः । प्र । राये । यंतु । शर्धंतः । अर्यः ॥

### Printed p. 160

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अयं द्व्यृचो दैवो मारुतो वा । उतापि च नोऽस्मदीयेष्वेषु नृषु पुरुषेषु श्रवोऽन्नं धुः । देवा मरुतो वा धारयंतु । राये धनार्थं शर्धंते उत्सहमानाः म्रियमाणा वार्योऽरयः प्र यंतु । प्रगच्छंतु । म्रियंतामित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This is a pair of verses (द्व्यृचो (*dvyṛco*)), addressed to the gods or to the Maruts. "Moreover, among these our men, let the gods or the Maruts establish food (श्रवः (*śravaḥ*)). For wealth (राये (*rāye*)), let the enemies who strive, making effort, go forth (प्र यंतु (*pra yaṃtu*))", that is, let them die.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उत (*uta*)—and
- (देवाः (*devāḥ*)—the gods)
- नः (*naḥ*)—belonging to us
- एषु नृषु (*eṣu nṛṣu*)—among these sacrificers
- श्रवः (*śravaḥ*)—food
- धुः (*dhuḥ*)—let them establish
- राये (*rāye*)—for our wealth
- शर्धंतः (*śardhaṃtaḥ*)—those who strive to seize it
- अर्यः (*aryaḥ*)—enemies
- प्र यंतु (*pra yaṃtu*)—let them go far away

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Among these sacrificers who belong to us, may the gods establish food. May the enemies who strive to seize our wealth go far away.
(Sāyaṇa has indicated that it can also be said that this mantra and the next are both addressed to the Maruts.)

##### English translation
May (the gods) bestow food upon our people : let foes contending for our riches perish.

#### Ṛk 19

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तपं॑ति॒ शत्रुं॒ स्व१॒॑र्ण भूमा॑ म॒हासे॑नासो॒ अमे॑भिरेषां ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तपंति । शत्रुं । स्वः । न । भूम । महासेनासः । अमेभिः । एषां ॥ १८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> महासेनासो महासेना राजान एषां मरुतां देवानां वामेभिर्बलैर्भूमा भुवनानि स्वर्णादित्य इव शत्रुं स्वकीयं तपंति । बाधंते । महांतोऽपि राजानोऽमैर्बलैः शत्रून्बाधंते । तानि बलानि देवानामेवेत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The महासेनासः (*mahāsenāsaḥ*) are kings of great armies. By the strengths (अमेभिः (*amebhiḥ*)) of these Maruts or gods, they afflict their own enemy, as the sun (स्वर्ण (*svarṇa*)) the regions (भूमा (*bhūmā*)). Even great kings oppress their enemies by these strengths, which belong to the gods alone.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- महासेनासः (*mahāsenāsaḥ*)—kings with very large armies
- एषां (*eṣāṃ*)—of these gods
- अमेभिः (*amebhiḥ*)—by the helps of their power
- भूमा (*bhūmā*)—all the worlds
- स्वर्न (*svarna*)—as the sun burns
- शत्रुं (*śatruṃ*)—their enemies
- तपंति (*tapaṃti*)—burn (harm)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As the sun burns all the worlds together, kings with very large armies burn their enemies by the help of the power of these gods. (They harm them.)

### Printed p. 161

##### English translation
Leaders of great armies, by the power of these (divinities), consume their foes, as the sun (scorches) the regions.

#### Ṛk 20

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ यन्नः॒ पत्नी॒र्गमं॒त्यच्छा॒ त्वष्टा॑ सुपा॒णिर्दधा॑तु वी॒रान् ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । यत् । नः । पत्नीः । गमंति । अच्छ । त्वष्टा । सुऽपाणिः । दधातु । वीरान् ॥ २० ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अस्यां देवपत्न्यस्त्वष्टा च देवता । यदा पत्नीर्देवानां पत्न्यो नो
> स्मानच्छाभ्या गमंति आगच्छंति तदा सुपाणिः शोभनहस्तस्त्वष्टा देवो वीरान् पुत्रान् दधातु । अस्मेभ्यं ददातु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In this verse the deities are the wives of the gods and Tvaṣṭṛ. When the wives of the gods come to us, then Tvaṣṭṛ, fair-handed (सुपाणिः (*supāṇiḥ*)), is to bestow heroes, that is sons, and give them to us.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत् (*yat*)—when
- पत्नीः (*patnīḥ*)—the wives of the gods
- नः अच्छ (*naḥ accha*)—facing us
- आ गमंति (*ā gamaṃti*)—come (then)
- सुपाणिः (*supāṇiḥ*)—having auspicious hands
- त्वष्टा (*tvaṣṭā*)—Tvaṣṭṛ
- वीरान् (*vīrān*)—heroic sons
- दधातु (*dadhātu*)—let him give to us

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

When the wives of the gods come facing us to our sacrifice, may Tvaṣṭṛ, whose hands are auspicious, give us heroic sons.

##### English translation
When the wives (of the gods) come before us, may the dextrous Twashtri grant us male progeny.

#### Ṛk 21

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्रति॑ नः॒ स्तोमं॒ त्वष्टा॑ जुषेत॒ स्याद॒स्मे अ॒रम॑तिर्वसू॒युः ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्रति । नः । स्तोमं । त्वष्टा । जुषेत । स्यात् । अस्मे इति । अरमतिः । वसुऽयुः ॥ २१ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> स्तोस्त्राकं स्तोमं स्तोत्रं त्वष्टा प्रति जुषेत । प्रतिसेवेत । अपि चारमतिः पर्याप्तबुद्धिः सर्वविषयव्यापिबुद्धिर्वा त्वष्टास्माकं अस्मदर्थं वसूयुर्धनेच्छावान् स्यात् । भूयात् ॥
> 21

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Let Tvaṣṭṛ be pleased with (प्रति जुषेत (*prati juṣeta*)) our praise (स्तोमं (*stomaṃ*)). Also let Tvaṣṭṛ, of sufficient (अरमतिः (*aramatiḥ*)) understanding, or of understanding that pervades all things, be desirous of wealth (वसूयुः (*vasūyuḥ*)) for us.

### Printed p. 162

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- स्तोमं (*stomaṃ*)—praise
- त्वष्टा (*tvaṣṭā*)—Tvaṣṭṛ
- प्रति जुषेत (*prati juṣeta*)—let him accept
- अरमतिः (*aramatiḥ*)—that same Tvaṣṭṛ, of full knowledge
- अस्मे (*asme*)—in our regard
- वसूयुः (*vasūyuḥ*)—desirous of wealth (to give to us)
- स्यात् (*syāt*)—let him be

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May Tvaṣṭṛ accept our praise. May that same Tvaṣṭṛ, of full knowledge, be desirous of wealth in order to give it to us.

##### English translation
May Twastri be propitiated by this our praise : may he who is of comprehensive understanding be inclined to give us wealth.

#### Ṛk 22

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ता नो॑ रासन्राति॒षाचो॒ वसू॒न्या रोद॑सी वरुणा॒नी शृ॑णोतु ।  
> वरू॑त्रीभिः सुशर॒णो नो॑ अस्तु॒ त्वष्टा॑ सु॒दत्रो॒ वि द॑धातु॒ रायः॑ ॥ २२ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ता । नः । रासन् । रातिऽसाचः । वसूनि । आ । रोदसी इति । वरुणानी । शृणोतु ।  
> वरूत्रीभिः । सुशरणः । नः । अस्तु । त्वष्टा । सुदत्रः । वि । दधातु । रायः ॥ २२ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ता यान्यस्माकमभीष्टानि तानि वसूनि धनानि रातिषाचो दानसमवेता देवपत्न्यो नोऽस्मभ्यं रासन् । प्रयच्छंतु । अपि च वरुणानी वरुणस्य पत्न्यै शृणोतु । अस्मदीयं स्तोत्रमभिशृणोतु । रोदसी द्यावापृथिव्यौ चाभिशृणुतां । सुदत्रः कल्याणदानस्त्वष्टा च वरूत्रीभिरुपद्रवाणां वारयित्रीभिर्देवपत्नीभिः सह नोऽस्मभ्यं सुशरणः सुशरणप्रदोऽस्तु । रायो धनानि च विदधातु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"Those desired things of ours, the riches (वसूनि (*vasūni*)), let the gift-accompanied (रातिषाचः (*rātiṣācaḥ*)) wives of the gods give (रासन् (*rāsan*)) to us. And let Varuṇānī, the wife of Varuṇa, hear our praise, and let Heaven and Earth (रोदसी (*rodasī*)) hear it. And Tvaṣṭṛ, of good gifts (सुदत्रः (*sudatraḥ*)), together with the wives of the gods who ward off (वरूत्रीभिः (*varūtrībhiḥ*)) calamities, be to us a giver of good refuge (सुशरणः (*suśaraṇaḥ*)), and let him bestow riches."

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- रातिषाचः (*rātiṣācaḥ*)—the wives of the gods, endowed with gifts
- ता (*tā*)—desired by us
- वसूनि (*vasūni*)—riches
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- रासन् (*rāsan*)—let them give
- वरुणानी (*varuṇānī*)—the wife of Varuṇa
- आ शृणोतु (*ā śṛṇotu*)—let her hear our praise
- रोदसी (*rodasī*)—let Heaven and Earth also hear
- सुदत्रः (*sudatraḥ*)—the excellent giver
- त्वष्टा (*tvaṣṭā*)—Tvaṣṭṛ
- वरूत्रीभिः (*varūtrībhiḥ*)—together with the wives of the gods who ward off calamities

### Printed p. 163

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- नमगॆ (*namagè*)—
- सुशरणः (*suśaraṇaḥ*)—as a giver of refuge
- अस्तु (*astu*)—may he be
- रायः (*rāyaḥ*)—riches
- वि दधातु (*vi dadhātu*)—may he provide

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May the goddess-wives of the gods, who are bestowers of gifts, give us the riches we desire. May the wife of Varuṇa hear our praise. May heaven and earth also hear our praise. May Tvaṣṭṛ, the excellent giver, together with the goddess-wives of the gods who ward off calamities, be a giver of refuge to us. And may he provide us with riches too.

##### English translation
May they who are the givers of gifts bestow upon us the treasures (we desire): may Rodasi and Varunani hear (our supplications): may the generous Twashtri, together with these (our) protectresses, be our sure refuge: may he give us riches.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Sudatraḥ—For this word Yāska gives the etymology: सुदत्रः कल्याणदानः । त्वष्टा सुदत्रो विदधातु रायः इत्यपि निगमो भवति (*sudatraḥ kalyāṇadānaḥ | tvaṣṭā sudatro vidadhātu rāyaḥ ityapi nigamo bhavati*) (Ni. 6-14). Sudatra means excellent giving; the sense is giving excellent things. In this matter he has cited the sentence of the present Ṛk itself, त्वष्टा सुदत्रो विदधातु रायः (*tvaṣṭā sudatro vidadhātu rāyaḥ*), as the example.

#### Ṛk 23

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तन्नो॒ रायः॒ पर्व॑ता॒स्तन्न॒ आप॒स्तद्रा॑ति॒षाच॒ ओष॑धीरु॒त द्यौः ।  
> वन॒स्पति॑भिः पृथि॒वी स॒जोषा॑ उ॒भे रोद॑सी॒ परि॑ पासतो नः ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तत् । नः । रायः । पर्वताः । तत् । नः । आपः । तत् । रातिऽसाचः । ओषधीः । उत । द्यौः ।  
> वनस्पतिभिः । पृथिवी । सजोषाः । उभे इति । रोदसी इति । परि । पासतः । नः ॥२३॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> नोऽस्माकं । तदित्यव्ययः । ता रायो धनानि पर्वताः परिपांतु । नोऽस्माकं तत्ता राय आपश्च परिपांतु । तद्रातिषाचो दानसहिता देवपत्न्यश्च परिपांतु । ओषधीरोषधयश्च तत्परिपांतु । उतापि च द्यौस्तत्परिपातु । वनस्पतिभिः सजोषाः सहिता पृथिव्यंतरिक्षं च तत्परिपातु । आपः पृथिवीत्यंतरिक्षनामसु पाठात् नोऽस्माकं तदुभे रोदसी द्यावापृथिव्यावपि परि पासतः । परिरक्षतां ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa construes the verse thus: नोऽस्माकं (*no 'smākaṃ*) means "our", and तत् (*tat*) is an indeclinable. May the mountains fully protect those riches; may the waters too protect for us those riches. May the goddess-wives of the gods, the givers of gifts, protect them, and the herbs likewise. May heaven protect them as well. May the earth, which is the atmosphere, united with the forest-trees, protect them; since आपः (*āpaḥ*) and पृथिवी (*pṛthivī*) are read among the names of the atmosphere, he takes them so. May both Rodasī, heaven and earth, also protect those riches for us.

### Printed p. 164

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- नः (*naḥ*)—for us
- तत् रायः (*tat rāyaḥ*)—that same desired wealth
- पर्वताः (*parvatāḥ*)—the mountains (may they protect fully)
- नः (*naḥ*)—for us
- तत् (*tat*)—that same wealth
- आपः (*āpaḥ*)—may the waters protect
- रातिषाचः (*rātiṣācaḥ*)—the goddess-wives of the gods devoted to giving
- तत् (*tat*)—may they protect it
- उत (*uta*)—and
- द्यौः (*dyauḥ*)—the heavenly world
- ओषधीः (*oṣadhīḥ*)—the herbs
- वनस्पतिभिः सजोषाः (*vanaspatibhiḥ sajoṣāḥ*)—united with the forest-trees
- पृथिवी (*pṛthivī*)—the atmosphere (may it protect)
- नः (*naḥ*)—for us
- उभे रोदसी (*ubhe rodasī*)—both heaven and earth
- परि पासतः (*pari pāsataḥ*)—may they protect those riches

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May the mountains and the waters protect the riches we desire. May the goddess-wives of the gods devoted to giving protect them too. May the presiding goddess of the heavenly world, the herbs, the atmosphere united with the forest-trees, (the earth) and heaven and earth as well, protect that wealth fully.

##### English translation
May the mountains, the waters, the liberal (wives of the gods), the plants, also the heaven and the earth, consentient with the forest lords and both the heaven and earth, preserve for us those (coveted) riches.

#### Ṛk 24

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अनु॒ तदु॒र्वी रोद॑सी जिहाता॒मनु॑ द्यु॒क्षो वरु॑ण॒ इंद्र॑सखा  
> अनु॒ विश्वे॑ म॒रुतो॒ ये स॒हासो॑ रा॒यः स्या॑म ध॒रुणं॑ धि॒यध्यै॑ ॥ २४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अनु । तत् । उर्वी इति । रोदसी इति । जिहातां । अनु । द्युक्षः । वरुणः । इंद्रसखा ।  
> अनु । विश्वे । मरुतः । ये । सहासः । रायः । स्याम । धरुणं । धियध्यै ॥ २४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> तद्वक्ष्यमाणमुर्वी विस्तीर्णे रोदसी द्यावापृथिव्यावनु जिहातां । अनुगच्छतां । अनुमन्येतामित्यर्थः । द्युक्षो दीप्तेर्निवासभूत इंद्रसखेंद्रसखः ॥ इंद्रः सखा यस्येति बहुव्रीहिः ॥ वरुणश्च तदनु जिहीतां । द्विवचनांतस्यैकवचनांततया विपरिणामः । ये सहासः शत्रूणामभिभवितारस्ते मरुतोऽपि तदनु जिहतां । अत्र बहुवचनांततया विपरिणामः । यदनुमंतव्यं तदाह । धियध्यै भरणीयं धारयितुं रायो धनस्य धरुणं धाम स्थानं वयं स्याम । भवेमेति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

May the vast heaven and earth, Rodasī, follow, that is, assent to, what is about to be said. May Varuṇa too, the dwelling-place of brilliance, Indra's friend (a bahuvrīhi: he whose friend is Indra), assent to it; the dual is changed to the singular. May the Maruts also, who are overpowerers of enemies (सहासः (*sahāsaḥ*)), assent; here the change is to the plural. He states what is to be assented to: that we may be the abode, the resting-place, of the wealth to be borne and held, धियध्यै (*dhiyadhyai*); may we become so.

### Printed p. 165

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- तत् (*tat*)—our desired objects
- उर्वी (*urvī*)—vast
- रोदसी (*rodasī*)—heaven and earth
- अनु जिहातां (*anu jihātāṃ*)—may they assent
- द्युक्षः (*dyukṣaḥ*)—the support of radiance
- इंद्रसखा (*iṃdrasakhā*)—friend of Indra
- वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*)—Varuṇa
- अनु (*anu*)—may he assent
- ये (*ye*)—who
- सहासः (*sahāsaḥ*)—are able to defeat enemies
- विश्वे (*viśve*)—all such
- मरुतः (*marutaḥ*)—Maruts
- अनु (*anu*)—may they assent
- (वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we)
- धियध्यै (*dhiyadhyai*)—for our sustenance
- रायः (*rāyaḥ*)—of wealth
- धरुणं (*dharuṇaṃ*)—as a dwelling-place
- स्याम (*syāma*)—may we become

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May heaven and earth assent to our desired objects. May Varuṇa, the support of radiance and friend of Indra, and all the Maruts, able to defeat enemies, assent. May we become the dwelling-place of wealth for our sustenance.

##### English translation
Let the vast heaven and earth consent: let the brilliant Varuna, of whom Indra is the friend, consent: let all the victorious Maruts consent that we may be a receptacle for the retention of riches.

#### Ṛk 25

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तन्न॒ इंद्रो॒ वरु॑णो मि॒त्रो अ॒ग्निराप॒ ओष॑धीर्व॒निनो॑ जुषंत  
> शर्म॑न्स्याम म॒रुता॑मु॒पस्थे॑ यू॒यं पा॑त स्व॒स्तिभिः॒ सदा॑ नः ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तत् । नः । इंद्रः । वरुणः । मित्रः । अग्निः । आपः । ओषधीः । वनिनः । जुषंत ।  
> शर्मन् । स्याम । मरुतां । उपस्थे । यूयं । पात । स्वस्तिभिः । सदा । नः ॥ २५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> नोऽस्मभ्यं तदिदं स्तोत्रमिंद्रो वरुणश्च मित्रश्चाग्निश्चापश्चौषधीरोषधयश्च वनिनो वृक्षाश्च जुषंत । जुषंतां । सेवंतां । वयं च मरुतामुपस्थ उपस्थाने वर्तमानाः शर्मन्शर्मणि सुखे गृहे वा स्याम । भवेम । सिद्धमन्यत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

May Indra, Varuṇa, Mitra, Agni, the waters, the herbs and the trees accept, that is, serve, this praise of ours. And may we, abiding in the lap (उपस्थ (*upastha*)) of the Maruts, be in शर्मन् (*śarman*), that is, in happiness or in a house. The rest is established.
